<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a separable real Banach space <i>Z</i> that contains no linear copy of <i>c</i><sub>0</sub>, yet is bi-Lipschitz equivalent to $`Z\oplus_\infty c_0`$. The same space contains a bi-Lipschitz image of every separable metric space.

- **claim-same-space-absorption** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a separable real Banach space $Z$ containing no closed linear subspace isomorphic to $c_0$, and an onto bi-Lipschitz map $$F:Z\oplus_\infty c_0\longrightarrow Z.$$
  - Explanation: A separable real Banach space absorbs a null-sequence summand through an onto bi-Lipschitz map while excluding a linear copy of that summand.
- **claim-lifted-correction-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-absorption)): Let $E,U,Z$ be real Banach spaces, let $D=E\oplus_\infty U$, and let $P(t,u)=u$. Suppose $Q:Z\to U$ is bounded linear, $g:D\to U$ is a bi-Lipschitz bijection, and $K:D\to Z$ is Lipschitz. Assume the exact identity $$QK(t,u)=g(t,u)-u \qquad ((t,u)\in D). \label{eq:absorb-identity}$$ Then $$F:Z\oplus_\infty E\longrightarrow Z,\qquad F(b,t)=b+K(t,Qb)$$ is a bi-Lipschitz bijection. If $$q=\\|Q\\|,\qquad L_K=\operatorname{Lip}(K),\qquad \\|g(x)-g(x')\\|_U\geq c_g\\|x-x'\\|_D$$ for some arbitrary lower constant $c_g>0$, then $$\operatorname{Lip}(F)\leq 1+L_K\max\{1,q\},\qquad \operatorname{Lip}(F^{-1})\leq \max\left\{1+\frac{L_Kq}{c_g},\frac{q}{c_g}\right\}. \label{eq:absorb-bounds}$$ No surjectivity or right-inverse assumption is imposed on $Q$.
  - Explanation: An exact lift of the shift correction gives a bi-Lipschitz bijection through a triangular formula. The linear correction operator need not be onto.
- **claim-local-coordinate-weights** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-coordinate-weights)): Let $\eta=10^{-3}$. There are continuous maps $$v^\lambda:B\to\mathbb R^{\Gamma_\lambda},\qquad w^\lambda:B\to(\mathbb R^{\Gamma_\lambda})^*$$ depending on finitely many coordinates of their argument, such that $$\\|v^\lambda(s)\\|_\infty\leq1,\qquad \\|w^\lambda(s)\\|_1\leq2,\qquad w^\lambda(s)v^\lambda(s)=1, \tag{W1}$$ and, for $s,s'\in B$, $$\min\{r_\lambda(s),r_\lambda(s')\} \max\!\left\{\\|v^\lambda(s)-v^\lambda(s')\\|_\infty, \\|w^\lambda(s)-w^\lambda(s')\\|_1\right\} \leq\eta\\|s-s'\\|_D. \tag{W2}$$ More precisely, the $j$th band of either weight is the product of a coefficient $\theta_j^{J_\lambda}(r_\lambda(s))$ and a vector or row depending only on $\pi_j s$. If $\theta_j^{J_\lambda}(r)>0$, then $$r\leq\rho_j\quad(j\geq1),\qquad r\geq\ell_j\quad(j<J_\lambda), \label{eq:band-support}$$ for positive constants $\rho_j,\ell_j$ defined in the proof.
  - Explanation: Finite-coordinate replacement weights have normalized pairing, uniform bounds, slow radius-weighted variation, and localized bands.
- **claim-correction-local-support** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-weight-support)): For every $\gamma=(\lambda,j,\xi)$, the function $f_\gamma$ is continuous, vanishes at $0$, and depends on a nonempty finite set $I_\gamma$ of coordinates of $D$. The sets can be chosen to include the first $j$ coordinates and every coordinate used by $r_\lambda$. Uniformly in $\gamma$, $$\label{eq:correction-bounds} \|f_\gamma(s)\|\leq4r_\lambda(s)\leq4,\qquad \operatorname{Lip}(f_\gamma)\leq4+12\eta.$$ Moreover $$f_\gamma(s)\ne0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \begin{cases} \\|\pi_j s-\xi\\|_\infty\leq2d_j,&j\geq1,\\ r_\lambda(s)\geq\ell_j,&j<J_\lambda, \end{cases} \label{eq:correction-support}$$ where both conditions are imposed when both apply.
  - Explanation: Each correction coordinate is continuous and finitely dependent, with uniform size and Lipschitz bounds and explicit cylinder and radial support restrictions.
- **claim-frozen-shift-isomorphism** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-frozen)): Choose for every $(i,n)$ a pair $v^{i,n},w^{i,n}$ satisfying (W1), independently between blocks. Define $S:D\to U$ by $$a_{i,0}=t_i,\qquad a_{i,n}=w^{i,n}x^{i,n},\qquad (Sx)^{i,n}=x^{i,n}+v^{i,n}(a_{i,n-1}-a_{i,n}). \label{eq:frozen-shift}$$ Then $S$ is a bounded linear bijection with $\\|S\\|\leq5$ and $\\|S^{-1}\\|\leq5$.
  - Explanation: Independently frozen replacement weights give a bounded linear bijection whose inverse recovers predecessor scalars from output readouts.
- **claim-nonlinear-global-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-global)): For every $x,x'\in D$, $$\left(\frac15-24\eta\right)\\|x-x'\\|_D \leq\\|g(x)-g(x')\\|_U \leq(5+24\eta)\\|x-x'\\|_D. \label{eq:global-bounds}$$ In particular, $g$ is injective and continuous.
  - Explanation: Slow variation of the weights preserves global distance bounds for the normalized nonlinear shift, establishing injectivity and continuity.
- **claim-nonlinear-shift-onto** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-onto)): The map $g:D\to U$ in <a href="#eq:g-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:g-definition">[eq:g-definition]</a> is onto. Together with Proposition <a href="#prop:global" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:global">6</a>, it is a bi-Lipschitz bijection.
  - Explanation: Finite rows with terminal scalars, a fixed-point argument, density, and closedness establish surjectivity of the nonlinear shift.
- **claim-local-operation-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-operations)): For every such $u,q,\varepsilon$, $$\begin{aligned} \\|R_{q,\varepsilon}u\\|_\infty &\leq\varepsilon\operatorname{Lip}(u),& \operatorname{Lip}(R_{q,\varepsilon}u)&\leq3\operatorname{Lip}(u),& \operatorname{Lip}(G_{q,\varepsilon}u)&\leq4\operatorname{Lip}(u), \label{eq:operation-lip}\\ \\|G_{q,\varepsilon}u\\|_\infty &\leq\\|u\\|_\infty,& \\|R_{q,\varepsilon}u\\|_\infty&\leq2\\|u\\|_\infty. \label{eq:operation-sup} \end{aligned}$$ Let $$K_I(u)=\overline{\{s\|_I:s\in B,\ u(s)\ne0\}}^{\,[-1,1]^I}.$$ In the next display write $G=G_{q,\varepsilon}$ and $R=R_{q,\varepsilon}$. With $K_\varepsilon=\{p:\operatorname{dist}_\infty(p,K)\leq \varepsilon\}$ and $\varnothing_\varepsilon=\varnothing$, one has $$K_I(Gu),K_I(Ru)\subset K_I(u)_\varepsilon,\qquad K_I(Ru)\subset\overline B_\infty(q,\varepsilon). \label{eq:operation-support}$$
  - Explanation: The plateau and remainder operations retain controlled Lipschitz and supremum bounds while enlarging support only by their scale; the remainder is also confined near its center.
- **claim-budgeted-support-propagation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-support)): Suppose $u\in\mathcal T_h$ has a representation based at $\gamma=(\lambda,j,\xi)$ with total scale $e=\sum_{k=1}^m\varepsilon_k$. On $\operatorname{supp}_B u$, $$\begin{cases} \\|\pi_j s-\xi\\|_\infty\leq2d_j+e,&j\geq1,\\ r_\lambda(s)\geq\ell_j-e,&j<J_\lambda. \end{cases} \label{eq:support-sharp}$$ In particular, on the same support, $$\begin{cases} \\|\pi_j s-\xi\\|_\infty\leq2d_j+\ell_j/2,&j\geq1,\\ r_\lambda(s)\geq\ell_j/2,&j<J_\lambda. \end{cases} \label{eq:support-coarse}$$
  - Explanation: An operation history enlarges the coordinate cylinder and decreases the radial threshold by at most its total scale. The strict budget retains a positive radial threshold.
- **claim-fixed-class-uniform-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-class-bounds)): With $A,L_0$ from <a href="#eq:base-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:base-bounds">[eq:base-bounds]</a>, the following bounds are uniform over all tests of the indicated class: $$\begin{array}{c\|cc} &\\|u\\|_\infty&\operatorname{Lip}(u)\\ \hline u\in\mathcal T_h&2^hA&4^hL_0\\ u\in\mathcal V_h&2^hA&4^hL_0+h4^hA. \end{array} \label{eq:class-bounds}$$
  - Explanation: Bounding operation depth and tail-factor complexity gives uniform supremum and Lipschitz bounds within each listed class.
- **claim-exact-completed-norm** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-completion)): Formula <a href="#eq:norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:norm">[eq:norm]</a> is a finite seminorm on $\mathcal M_0(B)$. Let $N$ be its kernel, and let $Z$ be the Banach completion of $\mathcal M_0(B)/N$ with the induced norm. Then $Z$ is separable, and $$\zeta:B\to Z,\qquad \zeta(s)=[\delta_s-\delta_0] \label{eq:zeta}$$ is $1$-Lipschitz with $\zeta(0)=0$. Each $\sigma_d$ descends and extends continuously to a seminorm on $Z$, and the exact formula $$\\|z\\|_Z^2=\sum_{d\geq1} \alpha_d\sigma_d(z)^2 \label{eq:completed-norm}$$ holds for every $z\in Z$.
  - Explanation: The finite molecule seminorm yields a separable Banach completion after quotienting its kernel. The point map is Lipschitz and the weighted seminorm formula remains exact after completion.
- **claim-fixed-class-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-duality)): Every test in $\mathcal T_h$ or $\mathcal V_h$ defines a bounded linear functional on $Z$ by its molecule pairing. For each fixed class $\mathcal L$, there is a finite common bound $B_{\mathcal L}$ for these dual norms. For each fixed triple $d=(\mathcal L,C,p)$, formula <a href="#eq:sigma" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sigma">[eq:sigma]</a> remains exact on $Z$, with the extended test pairings.* *If $u_n\in\mathcal L$ and $u_n(s)\to u(s)$ for every $s\in B$, then the pointwise limit defines a bounded functional on $Z$ of norm at most $B_{\mathcal L}$, and $\langle u_n,z\rangle\to\langle u,z\rangle$ for each fixed $z\in Z$. More precisely, for any molecule class $m$ and any $z\in Z$, $$\|\langle u_n-u,z\rangle\| \leq\|\langle u_n-u,m\rangle\| +2B_{\mathcal L}\\|z-m\\|_Z. \label{eq:fixed-vector}$$
  - Explanation: Tests extend to bounded dual functionals with a common bound in each fixed class. Pointwise limits converge on each fixed vector, and admissible-list formulas remain exact.
- **claim-linear-correction-operator** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-q)): There is a bounded linear map $Q:Z\to U$ such that $$Q\zeta(s)=h(s)=(S_s-P)s \qquad(s\in B). \label{eq:Qzeta}$$
  - Explanation: The correction coordinates define a bounded linear operator into the null-sequence target, reproducing the correction on point molecules.
- **claim-radial-lipschitz-lift** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-k)): Define $$K(0)=0,\qquad K(x)=\\|x\\|_D\,\zeta\!\left(\frac{x}{\\|x\\|_D}\right) \quad(x\ne0). \label{eq:K}$$ Then $K:D\to Z$ is positively homogeneous and $\operatorname{Lip}(K)\leq3$. It satisfies the exact identity $$QK=g-P. \label{eq:lift}$$
  - Explanation: Radially extending the point-molecule map gives a positively homogeneous Lipschitz lift satisfying the exact correction identity.
- **claim-single-class-detectors** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-detectors)): Suppose $(m_i)\subset Z$ satisfies, for some $r,M>0$, $$\\|m_i\\|_Z\geq r,\qquad \left\\|\sum_{i\in H}\epsilon_i m_i\right\\|_Z\leq M \quad\text{for all finite \(H\) and all signs \(\epsilon_i\)}. \label{eq:bounded-signed}$$ Then $u(m_i)\to0$ for every $u\in Z^*$. After a subsequence, there are tests $F_i$ in one listed class $\mathcal L$ and a constant $\delta>0$ with $$\|\langle F_i,m_i\rangle\|\geq\delta. \label{eq:detected}$$
  - Explanation: A sequence bounded away from zero with uniformly bounded finite signed sums is weakly null and has scalar detectors from one fixed class after passage to a subsequence.
- **claim-compatible-detector-growth** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-detector-growth)): Let $(m_i)\subset Z$, and let $F_i$ belong to one listed class $\mathcal L$. Suppose there are $\delta>0$ and an integer $C\geq1$ such that $$\|\langle F_i,m_i\rangle\|\geq\delta, \qquad \operatorname{Lip}\!\left(\sum_{i\in H}\epsilon_iF_i\right)\leq C$$ for every $i$, every finite $H$, and all signs $\epsilon_i$. For the listed triple $d=(\mathcal L,C,2)$, every nonempty finite $H$ satisfies $$\max_{\epsilon_i\in\{-1,1\}} \left\\|\sum_{i\in H}\epsilon_i m_i\right\\|_Z \geq\sqrt{\alpha_d}\,\delta\sqrt{\|H\|}. \label{eq:detector-growth}$$
  - Explanation: Uniform detection by compatible tests forces finite signed sums of the detected vectors to grow with the square root of the size of the selected set.
- **claim-support-compatibility** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-compatible)): Let $B$ be a convex subset of a normed space. Let $u_i,b_i$ be continuous real functions on $B$, with $b_i$ Lipschitz, and put $F_i=u_i b_i$. Suppose $\operatorname{Lip}(F_i)\leq L$ for every $i$.* 1. *If the closed relative supports $\operatorname{supp}_B F_i$ are pairwise disjoint, every finite signed sum of the $F_i$ has Lipschitz constant at most $L$.* 2. *Suppose that for each $i$ there is a relatively open set $C_i\subset B$ and a scalar $e_i$ such that $u_i=e_i$ on $C_i$ and $\operatorname{supp}_B u_k\subset C_i$ for every $k>i$. If $\sum_i\|e_i\|\operatorname{Lip}(b_i)<\infty$, every finite signed sum of the $F_i$ has Lipschitz constant at most $$L+\sum_i\|e_i\|\operatorname{Lip}(b_i).$$ If $b_i=1$ for every $i$, the summability condition is automatic.
  - Explanation: Disjoint supports or nested regions of constancy yield a common Lipschitz bound for finite signed sums. Tail factors in the nested case require summable plateau contributions.
- **claim-tail-separated-detectors** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-tail-separation)): Suppose $F_i$ belong to one listed class and have representations with bounded band indices. Suppose $m_i\in Z$ and $$\|\langle F_i,m_i\rangle\|\geq\delta>0,\qquad \operatorname{supp}_B F_i\subset\{\tau_{M_i}\geq c_*\}, \quad M_i\in\mathbb N,\quad M_i\to\infty,\quad c_*>0. \label{eq:tail-hypotheses}$$ Then a subsequence has modified tests in one class $\mathcal V_H$, with detection at least $\delta/2$, whose relative supports are pairwise disjoint.
  - Explanation: Detectors supported on escaping tails with a common positive threshold can be modified within one enlarged class to have pairwise disjoint supports while retaining detection.
- **claim-compatible-detector-selection** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-compatible-detectors)): Let $(m_i)\subset Z$ be weakly null, meaning $u(m_i)\to0$ for every $u\in Z^*$. Suppose $F_i$ belong to one listed class $\mathcal L$ and $$\|\langle F_i,m_i\rangle\|\geq\delta>0 \qquad(i\geq1). \label{eq:selection-detection}$$ There are a subsequence $(m_{i_k})$, a listed class $\mathcal L_1$, tests $\widetilde F_k\in\mathcal L_1$, $\delta_1>0$, and an integer $C_1\geq1$ such that $$\|\langle\widetilde F_k,m_{i_k}\rangle\|\geq\delta_1, \qquad \operatorname{Lip}\!\left(\sum_{k\in H}\epsilon_k\widetilde F_k\right)\leq C_1$$ for every $k$, every finite $H$, and all signs $\epsilon_k$.
  - Explanation: Weak nullity and detection within a fixed class allow subsequence selection and local modifications that produce compatible detectors with a positive common pairing.
- **claim-no-linear-null-sequences** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-no-c0)): The Banach space $Z$ contains no linearly isomorphic copy of $c_0$.
  - Explanation: An assumed linear null-sequence copy would provide bounded signed sums, whereas compatible detectors force unbounded growth.
- **claim-same-space-universality** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-cor-same-space)): For this same separable real Banach space $Z$:* 1. *$Z\oplus_\infty c_0$ and $Z$ are bi-Lipschitz equivalent but not linearly isomorphic.* 2. *$c_0$ embeds bi-Lipschitzly into $Z$, although $Z$ contains no linear copy of $c_0$.* 3. *Every separable metric space embeds bi-Lipschitzly into $Z$.
  - Explanation: The same space gives nonlinear absorption without linear isomorphism, contains a bi-Lipschitz null-sequence image, and is a bi-Lipschitz target for every separable metric space.

        "exact_quote": "For every such $u,q,\\varepsilon$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|R_{q,\\varepsilon}u\\|_\\infty\n   &\\leq\\varepsilon\\operatorname{Lip}(u),&\n \\operatorname{Lip}(R_{q,\\varepsilon}u)&\\leq3\\operatorname{Lip}(u),&\n \\operatorname{Lip}(G_{q,\\varepsilon}u)&\\leq4\\operatorname{Lip}(u), \\label{eq:operation-lip}\\\\\n \\|G_{q,\\varepsilon}u\\|_\\infty\n   &\\leq\\|u\\|_\\infty,&\n \\|R_{q,\\varepsilon}u\\|_\\infty&\\leq2\\|u\\|_\\infty. \\label{eq:operation-sup}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Let $$K_I(u)=\\overline{\\{s|_I:s\\in B,\\ u(s)\\ne0\\}}^{\\,[-1,1]^I}.$$ In the next display write $G=G_{q,\\varepsilon}$ and $R=R_{q,\\varepsilon}$. With $K_\\varepsilon=\\{p:\\operatorname{dist}_\\infty(p,K)\\leq\n\\varepsilon\\}$ and $\\varnothing_\\varepsilon=\\varnothing$, one has $$K_I(Gu),K_I(Ru)\\subset K_I(u)_\\varepsilon,\\qquad\n K_I(Ru)\\subset\\overline B_\\infty(q,\\varepsilon).             \\label{eq:operation-support}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-324.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-support",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $u\\in\\mathcal T_h$ has a representation based at $\\gamma=(\\lambda,j,\\xi)$ with total scale $e=\\sum_{k=1}^m\\varepsilon_k$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-class-bounds",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-completion",
        "exact_quote": "Formula <a href=\"#eq:norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:norm\">[eq:norm]</a> is a finite seminorm on $\\mathcal M_0(B)$. Let $N$ be its kernel, and let $Z$ be the Banach completion of $\\mathcal M_0(B)/N$ with the induced norm. Then $Z$ is separable, and $$\\zeta:B\\to Z,\\qquad \\zeta(s)=[\\delta_s-\\delta_0]             \\label{eq:zeta}$$ is $1$-Lipschitz with $\\zeta(0)=0$. Each $\\sigma_d$ descends and extends continuously to a seminorm on $Z$, and the exact formula $$\\|z\\|_Z^2=\\sum_{d\\geq1}\n                                \\alpha_d\\sigma_d(z)^2         \\label{eq:completed-norm}$$ holds for every $z\\in Z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-324.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-completion",
          "exact_quote": "Formula <a href=\"#eq:norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:norm\">[eq:norm]</a> is a finite seminorm on $\\mathcal M_0(B)$. Then $Z$ is separable, and $$\\zeta:B\\to Z,\\qquad \\zeta(s)=[\\delta_s-\\delta_0]             \\label{eq:zeta}$$ is $1$-Lipschitz with $\\zeta(0)=0$. Each $\\sigma_d$ descends and extends continuously to a seminorm on $Z$, and the exact formula $$\\|z\\|_Z^2=\\sum_{d\\geq1}\n                                \\alpha_d\\sigma_d(z)^2         \\label{eq:completed-norm}$$ holds for every $z\\in Z$.",
          "source_id": "PM-324.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Formula <a href=\"#eq:norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:norm\">[eq:norm]</a> is a finite seminorm on $\\mathcal M_0(B)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-duality",
        "exact_quote": "Every test in $\\mathcal T_h$ or $\\mathcal V_h$ defines a bounded linear functional on $Z$ by its molecule pairing. For each fixed class $\\mathcal L$, there is a finite common bound $B_{\\mathcal L}$ for these dual norms. For each fixed triple $d=(\\mathcal L,C,p)$, formula <a href=\"#eq:sigma\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sigma\">[eq:sigma]</a> remains exact on $Z$, with the extended test pairings.*\n\n\n*If $u_n\\in\\mathcal L$ and $u_n(s)\\to u(s)$ for every $s\\in B$, then the pointwise limit defines a bounded functional on $Z$ of norm at most $B_{\\mathcal L}$, and $\\langle u_n,z\\rangle\\to\\langle u,z\\rangle$ for each fixed $z\\in Z$. More precisely, for any molecule class $m$ and any $z\\in Z$, $$|\\langle u_n-u,z\\rangle|\n \\leq|\\langle u_n-u,m\\rangle|\n       +2B_{\\mathcal L}\\|z-m\\|_Z.                            \\label{eq:fixed-vector}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-324.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-detectors",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $(m_i)\\subset Z$ satisfies, for some $r,M>0$, $$\\|m_i\\|_Z\\geq r,\\qquad\n \\left\\|\\sum_{i\\in H}\\epsilon_i m_i\\right\\|_Z\\leq M\n \\quad\\text{for all finite \\(H\\) and all signs \\(\\epsilon_i\\)}. \\label{eq:bounded-signed}$$ Then $u(m_i)\\to0$ for every $u\\in Z^*$.         "source_id": "PM-324.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Bi-Lipschitz embedding** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A map $F:X\to Y$ is a *bi-Lipschitz embedding* if there are constants $0<c\leq C<\infty$ such that $$c\\|x-x'\\|_X\leq\\|F(x)-F(x')\\|_Y \leq C\\|x-x'\\|_X\qquad(x,x'\in X).$$
- **Bi-Lipschitz equivalence** ([source](#sec:introduction)): It is a *bi-Lipschitz equivalence* when it is also onto $Y$.
- **Null-sequence space** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The space $c_0=c_0(\mathbb N)$ consists of real scalar sequences tending to zero, with the supremum norm.
- **Supremum direct sum** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For Banach spaces $X,Y$, write $X\oplus_\infty Y$ for their product with norm $\\|(x,y)\\|=\max\{\\|x\\|,\\|y\\|\}$.
- **Normalized state-dependent shift** ([source](#eq:g-definition)): Define $$g(0)=0,\qquad g(x)=S_{s_x}x,\quad s_x=\frac{x}{\\|x\\|_D}\quad(x\ne0). \label{eq:g-definition}$$
- **Ordered scale budget** ([source](#eq:scale-rule)): For $m\geq1$, the scales in their order of application must satisfy $$\varepsilon_1\geq\varepsilon_2\geq\cdots\geq\varepsilon_m>0, \qquad \sum_{k=1}^m\varepsilon_k<\frac{\ell_j}{2}. \label{eq:scale-rule}$$
- **Relative support** ([source](#pm-h-11)): For a continuous function on $B$, let $\operatorname{supp}_B u=\overline{\{s\in B:u(s)\ne0\}}^{\,B}$.
- **Mass-zero finite molecules** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Let $\mathcal M_0(B)$ be the real vector space of mass-zero finite formal sums $$m=\sum_{s\in B}a_s\delta_s,\qquad \#\{s:a_s\ne0\}<\infty,\qquad \sum_s a_s=0.$$
- **Molecule pairing** ([source](#pm-h-13)): For a real function $u$ on $B$, set $\langle u,m\rangle=\sum_s a_su(s)$.
- **Compatible sequence of tests** ([source](#pm-h-18)): Call a sequence of tests *compatible* if all its finite signed sums have a common Lipschitz bound.
- **Weakly null sequence** ([source](#stmt-prop-compatible-detectors)): Let $(m_i)\subset Z$ be weakly null, meaning $u(m_i)\to0$ for every $u\in Z^*$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-weight-variation-parameter | Weight variation parameter | = | $10^{-3}$ | Fixed throughout the construction. |
| q-frozen-forward-norm | Frozen shift operator norm | ≤ | $5$ | Uniform over independently chosen frozen weights satisfying the stated pairing and norm conditions. |
| q-frozen-inverse-norm | Frozen inverse operator norm | ≤ | $5$ | Uniform over the permitted frozen weights. |
| q-global-lower-distance | Output distance divided by input distance for distinct inputs | ≥ | $\frac15-24\eta$ | All distinct input pairs, with the fixed weight variation parameter. |
| q-global-upper-distance | Output distance divided by input distance for distinct inputs | ≤ | $5+24\eta$ | All distinct input pairs. |
| q-base-supremum-bound | Common supremum norm of correction coordinates | ≤ | $4$ | Uniform over correction labels. |
| q-base-lipschitz-bound | Common Lipschitz bound of correction coordinates | ≤ | $4+12\eta$ | Uniform over correction labels. |
| q-remainder-lipschitz-bound | Lipschitz constant of a local remainder | ≤ | $3\operatorname{Lip}(u)$ | Permitted centers and dyadic scales. |
| q-plateau-lipschitz-bound | Lipschitz constant after a plateau operation | ≤ | $4\operatorname{Lip}(u)$ | All permitted local operations. |
| q-cylindrical-class-lipschitz | Lipschitz constant of a cylindrical test | ≤ | $4^hL_0$ | A fixed operation budget. |
| q-tail-class-lipschitz | Lipschitz constant of a test with tail factors | ≤ | $4^hL_0+h4^hA$ | A fixed class budget. |
| q-point-map-lipschitz | Lipschitz constant of the point-molecule map | ≤ | $1$ | All point pairs in the unit ball. |
| q-radial-lift-lipschitz | Lipschitz constant of the radial lift | ≤ | $3$ | The radial extension of the point-molecule map. |
| q-compatible-detector-growth | Maximum norm of a finite signed sum of detected vectors | ≥ | $\sqrt{\alpha_d}\,\delta\sqrt{\|H\|}$ | Every nonempty finite selected set, for the specified listed triple. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-absorption-algebra** [claim](#stmt-lem-absorption) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-absorption): Recover the triangular input from the shift inverse and verify both compositions. -> A bi-Lipschitz absorption bijection with inverse bounds.
- **step-coordinate-weights** [claim](#stmt-lem-coordinate-weights) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coordinate-weights): Normalize finite-grid bump weights and interpolate bands using a logarithmic radial schedule. -> Replacement weights with normalized pairing and controlled variation.
- **step-correction-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-weight-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weight-support): Identify the finite dependency set and compare endpoint corrections through independently frozen weights. -> Uniform correction bounds and localized supports.
- **step-frozen-inverse** [claim](#stmt-lem-frozen) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frozen): Read predecessor scalars from output blocks and reconstruct the input explicitly. -> A bounded linear shift bijection and bounded inverse.
- **step-global-shift-bounds** [claim](#stmt-prop-global) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-global): Compare normalized endpoint states to a common blockwise frozen shift and bound the perturbation. -> Positive global lower and finite upper distance bounds.
- **step-shift-surjectivity** [claim](#stmt-prop-onto) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-onto): Add terminal scalars to finite rows, apply a fixed-point theorem, eliminate spillover, and use density and closedness. -> Surjectivity of the nonlinear shift.
- **step-local-operation-estimates** [claim](#stmt-lem-operations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-operations): Express the remainder as a cutoff times a local increment and track new nonzero values. -> Lipschitz, supremum, and support estimates for both local operations.
- **step-support-propagation** [claim](#stmt-lem-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-support): Induct over operation histories and then take relative closures. -> Cylinder and radial support bounds for generated tests.
- **step-class-estimates** [claim](#stmt-lem-class-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-class-bounds): Iterate local-operation bounds and telescope products of tail factors. -> Uniform bounds within each listed class.
- **step-molecule-completion** [claim](#stmt-lem-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-completion): Bound point differences, quotient the seminorm kernel, and pass the weighted square-sum identity through completion. -> The separable Banach space, its exact norm, and its Lipschitz point map.
- **step-test-duality** [claim](#stmt-lem-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-duality): Use admissible singleton and finite lists, then approximate each fixed completed vector by molecules. -> Uniform dual bounds, exact list formulas, and fixed-vector convergence.
- **step-linear-correction-map** [claim](#stmt-lem-q) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-q): Sum the correction on finite molecules and control all coordinates by one admissible singleton seminorm. -> A bounded linear correction operator.
- **step-radial-lift** [claim](#stmt-lem-k) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-k): Extend the point map radially and combine normalization control with the correction identity on the unit ball. -> A Lipschitz lift of the exact shift correction.
- **step-detector-extraction** [claim](#stmt-lem-detectors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detectors): Use sign matching for weak nullity, a square-sum gliding hump to fix a seminorm, and a smaller exponent to extract scalar detection. -> A weakly null subsequence with detectors in one listed class.
- **step-detector-growth** [claim](#stmt-lem-detector-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detector-growth): Average over independent signs so cross terms cancel and retain the diagonal pairings. -> Growth of maximum signed-sum norms.
- **step-support-compatibility** [claim](#stmt-lem-compatible) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compatible): Establish local Lipschitz bounds from disjoint supports or nested plateaux, then subdivide segments in the convex domain. -> Uniform Lipschitz bounds for all finite signed sums.
- **step-tail-separation** [claim](#stmt-lem-tail-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tail-separation): Multiply each fixed detector by a sufficiently late tail cutoff and recursively separate upper and lower tail requirements. -> Disjoint modified detectors in one enlarged class.
- **step-compatible-selection** [claim](#stmt-prop-compatible-detectors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-compatible-detectors): Separate distinct bounded-band labels by tails, nest plateaux for growing bands, and expand repeated-label tests to isolate escaping tails or small remainders. -> Compatible detectors with a retained positive common pairing.
- **step-exclude-linear-copy** [claim](#stmt-prop-no-c0) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-no-c0): Apply detector extraction, compatible selection, and growth to an assumed image of the null-sequence basis. -> A contradiction excluding the linear copy.
- **step-main-assembly** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-no-c0-2): Apply the absorption criterion to the completed space, the onto shift, and the exact Lipschitz lift. -> The whole-space bi-Lipschitz absorption counterexample.
- **step-same-space-consequences** [claim](#stmt-cor-same-space) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-same-space): Restrict the absorption map to the direct summand and compose with the cited separable metric embedding theorem. -> Failure of linear isomorphism and same-space metric universality.

          "exact_quote": "For $J=J_\\lambda$ and $r=r_\\lambda(s)$, concatenate the bands: $$v^\\lambda(s)\n   =\\bigl(\\theta_j^J(r)v^j(s)\\bigr)_{j=0}^J,\n \\qquad\n w^\\lambda(s)\n   =\\bigl(\\theta_j^J(r)w^j(s)\\bigr)_{j=0}^J.$$",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-weight-support",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-weight-support</summary>\n\n*Proof.* For $n>1$, $a_{i,n-1}$ uses only $s^{i,n-1}$, the local radius $r_{i,n-1}(s)$, and $\\pi_{J_{i,n-1}}s$. The former radius uses $s^{i,n-2}$ when $n>2$ and $t_i$ when $n=2$. The current scalar $a_{i,n}$ uses only $s^{i,n}$, $r_{i,n}(s)$, and $\\pi_{J_{i,n}}s$; for $n=1$ the predecessor scalar is $t_i$. Since $J_{i,n-1}<J_{i,n}$, one possible common finite set for all coordinates $\\gamma$ at $\\lambda=(i,n)$ is the union of the first $J_\\lambda$ enumerated coordinates, the coordinate $t_i$, and every coordinate of the blocks $\\Gamma_{i,k}$ with $k\\in\\{n,n-1,n-2\\}\\cap\\mathbb N$. Call it $I_\\gamma$. It is nonempty and contains the coordinates required in the statement.\n\nThe row bound (W1) gives $|a_{i,n-1}(s)-a_{i,n}(s)|\\leq4r_\\lambda(s)$, also when $n=1$. Thus $|f_\\gamma(s)|\\leq4r_\\lambda(s)\\leq4$ and $f_\\gamma(0)=0$. To verify the uniform Lipschitz bound, fix $s,s'\\in B$ and write $\\delta=\\|s-s'\\|_D$. At each level choose the pair $(\\bar v^\\lambda,\\bar w^\\lambda)$ from whichever of $s,s'$ has the larger local radius. If $e\\in\\{s,s'\\}$ is the other endpoint, (W2) gives $$r_\\lambda(e)\\max\\!\\left\\{\n    \\|v^\\lambda(e)-\\bar v^\\lambda\\|_\\infty,\n    \\|w^\\lambda(e)-\\bar w^\\lambda\\|_1\\right\\}\n \\leq\\eta\\delta;$$ the same bound is trivial if $e$ supplied the frozen pair. For $e=s,s'$, replacing its weights by the frozen weights in the correction vector $v^\\lambda(e)(a_{i,n-1}(e)-a_{i,n}(e))$ costs at most $4\\eta\\delta$ from the vector and at most $2\\eta\\delta$ from its current and predecessor rows together. At $n=1$ the predecessor $t_i$ does not change. With all weights frozen, the correction vector is linear in the input and has norm at most $4r_\\lambda(s-s')\\leq4\\delta$. Comparing the two endpoints therefore gives $|f_\\gamma(s)-f_\\gamma(s')|\n\\leq(4+12\\eta)\\delta$.\n\nIf $f_\\gamma(s)\\ne0$, its displayed product has both $\\theta_j^{J_\\lambda}(r_\\lambda(s))>0$ and $v^j_\\xi(s)>0$. For $j\\geq1$, the formula for $v^j_\\xi$ gives $\\|\\pi_j s-\\xi\\|_\\infty<2d_j$, which implies the first closed inequality in <a href=\"#eq:correction-support\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:correction-support\">[eq:correction-support]</a>. If $j<J_\\lambda$, the second inequality follows from <a href=\"#eq:band-support\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:band-support\">[eq:band-support]</a>. This includes $j=0$; no grid condition is imposed there. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-324.2"
        }
      ],

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-operations",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-operations",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-operations</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Set $a(s)=1-\\chi(\\|s|_I-q\\|_\\infty/\\varepsilon)$. Then $$R_{q,\\varepsilon}u(s)=a(s)(u(q)-u(s)),\\qquad\n 0\\leq a\\leq1,\\qquad \\operatorname{Lip}(a)\\leq2/\\varepsilon.$$ If $a(s)\\ne0$, then $\\|s|_I-q\\|_\\infty<\\varepsilon$; the cylindrical Lipschitz bound gives $|u(q)-u(s)|\\leq\\varepsilon\\operatorname{Lip}(u)$. This proves the first supremum estimate in <a href=\"#eq:operation-lip\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:operation-lip\">[eq:operation-lip]</a>.\n\nTo compare $Ru(s)$ and $Ru(t)$, there is nothing to prove if both values of $a$ are zero. Otherwise interchange $s,t$, if needed, so that $a(t)\\ne0$. The identity $$Ru(s)-Ru(t)\n =a(s)(u(t)-u(s))+(a(s)-a(t))(u(q)-u(t))$$ bounds the two terms by $\\operatorname{Lip}(u)\\|s-t\\|_D$ and $(2/\\varepsilon)\\|s-t\\|_D\\,\\varepsilon\\operatorname{Lip}(u)$, respectively. Thus $\\operatorname{Lip}(Ru)\\leq3\\operatorname{Lip}(u)$, and $Gu=u+Ru$ gives $\\operatorname{Lip}(Gu)\\leq4\\operatorname{Lip}(u)$. The formula for $G$ is a convex combination of $u(q)$ and $u(s)$, so it does not increase the supremum norm. The bound for $R=G-u$ follows.\n\nFor the support assertions, if a new output is nonzero at $s$ while $u(s)=0$, then $u(q)\\ne0$ and $\\|s|_I-q\\|_\\infty<\\varepsilon$. If $u(s)\\ne0$, its projection already lies in $K_I(u)$. This covers both $G$ and $R$. Taking closures gives their inclusion in $K_I(u)_\\varepsilon$; the latter set is closed because the cube is compact. In addition, $Ru(s)=0$ whenever $\\|s|_I-q\\|_\\infty\\geq\\varepsilon$, which proves its ball inclusion. If $u=0$, all statements are immediate. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-324.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-local-operation-estimates",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite-coordinate Lipschitz functions",
        "Permitted grid centers and scales"
      ],
      "method": "Express the remainder as a cutoff times a local increment and track new nonzero values.",
      "output": "Lipschitz, supremum, and support estimates for both local operations.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-operations"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-support",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-support",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-support</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Start with <a href=\"#eq:correction-support\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:correction-support\">[eq:correction-support]</a>, which holds on the relative support of $f_\\gamma$ as well as its nonzero set, because its two inequalities describe closed subsets of $B$. Suppose the claim has been proved for the old function and add an operation of scale $\\varepsilon$. If the new function is nonzero at $s$ and the old one is zero there, the proof of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:operations\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:operations\">8</a> gives an old nonzero value at the zero extension of $q$, with $\\|s|_{I_\\gamma}-q\\|_\\infty<\\varepsilon$. Otherwise the old support already contains $s$. Both $\\pi_j$ and $r_\\lambda$ depend only on $I_\\gamma$; coordinate restriction has norm one, and $r_\\lambda$ is $1$-Lipschitz on that cube. The first upper bound therefore increases by at most $\\varepsilon$, and the second lower bound decreases by at most $\\varepsilon$. Induction proves <a href=\"#eq:support-sharp\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:support-sharp\">[eq:support-sharp]</a> on the nonzero set. Taking relative closure preserves the closed inequalities. Finally, <a href=\"#eq:scale-rule\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scale-rule\">[eq:scale-rule]</a> gives <a href=\"#eq:support-coarse\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:support-coarse\">[eq:support-coarse]</a>. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-324.2"
        }
      ],

      ],
      "method": "Induct over operation histories and then take relative closures.",
      "output": "Cylinder and radial support bounds for generated tests.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-support"

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-class-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-class-bounds</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Iterating <a href=\"#eq:operation-lip\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:operation-lip\">[eq:operation-lip]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:operation-sup\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:operation-sup\">[eq:operation-sup]</a> gives the first row. For the second, every tail factor has supremum norm at most one and Lipschitz constant at most $2^h$. A product of at most $h$ such factors has Lipschitz constant at most $h2^h$, by telescoping the product difference. The product rule then gives $$\\operatorname{Lip}\\!\\left(u\\prod_r\\beta_r\\right)\n \\leq4^hL_0+(2^hA)(h2^h)=4^hL_0+h4^hA.$$ Its supremum norm is at most $2^hA$. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-324.2"
        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-q",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-q</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Define on formal molecules $$Q_0m=\\sum_s a_s h(s)\\in U.$$ This finite sum lies in $U$, not merely in $\\ell_\\infty$, because each $h(s)$ lies in $U$. Each coordinate function $f_\\gamma$ belongs to $\\mathcal T_1$ through its zero-operation representation. Choose an integer $C_0\\geq L_0$ and the triple $d_0=(\\mathcal T_1,C_0,2)$. Every singleton $(f_\\gamma)$ is admissible, whence $$\\|Q_0m\\|_U=\\sup_\\gamma|\\langle f_\\gamma,m\\rangle|\n \\leq\\sigma_{d_0}(m)\\leq\\alpha_{d_0}^{-1/2}\\|m\\|_0.$$ Thus $Q_0$ vanishes on $N$, descends, and extends to a bounded linear map into the complete space $U$. Since $h(0)=0$, applying it to $\\delta_s-\\delta_0$ proves <a href=\"#eq:Qzeta\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:Qzeta\">[eq:Qzeta]</a>. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-324.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-linear-correction-map",
      "inputs": [

      "output": "A bounded linear correction operator.",

    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-k",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-molecule-completion",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-k",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-k</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Since $\\zeta(0)=0$ and $\\operatorname{Lip}(\\zeta)\\leq1$, $\\|\\zeta(s)\\|_Z\\leq1$ for $s\\in B$. Suppose $a=\\|x\\|_D\\geq b=\\|x'\\|_D>0$, and write $s=x/a$, $s'=x'/b$. Splitting at $b\\zeta(s)$ gives $$\\|K(x)-K(x')\\|_Z\n \\leq(a-b)\\|\\zeta(s)\\|_Z+b\\|\\zeta(s)-\\zeta(s')\\|_Z\n \\leq\\|x-x'\\|_D+b\\|s-s'\\|_D.$$ The normalization estimate <a href=\"#eq:normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:normalization\">[eq:normalization]</a> bounds the last term by $2\\|x-x'\\|_D$. If an endpoint is zero, use $\\|K(x)\\|_Z\\leq\\|x\\|_D$. This proves the Lipschitz bound. For $x\\ne0$, <a href=\"#eq:Qzeta\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:Qzeta\">[eq:Qzeta]</a>, <a href=\"#eq:h-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:h-definition\">[eq:h-definition]</a>, and <a href=\"#eq:g-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:g-definition\">[eq:g-definition]</a> give $$QK(x)=\\|x\\|_D h(x/\\|x\\|_D)\n      =S_{x/\\|x\\|_D}x-Px=g(x)-Px.$$ All terms vanish at zero, and positive homogeneity follows directly from <a href=\"#eq:K\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:K\">[eq:K]</a>. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-324.2"
        }
      ],

      "output": "A Lipschitz lift of the exact shift correction.",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-detector-growth",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-detector-growth",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-detector-growth</summary>\n\n*Proof.* The list $(F_k)_{k\\in H}$ is admissible for $d$, including when labels repeat. For independent real random signs, the exact list formula in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:duality\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:duality\">12</a> gives $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E\\,\\sigma_d\\!\\left(\\sum_{i\\in H}\\epsilon_i m_i\\right)^2\n &\\geq\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{k\\in H}\n       \\left|\\sum_{i\\in H}\\epsilon_i\n                    \\langle F_k,m_i\\rangle\\right|^2\\\\\n &=\\sum_{k\\in H}\\sum_{i\\in H}|\\langle F_k,m_i\\rangle|^2\n \\geq |H|\\delta^2.\n\\end{aligned}$$ The equality uses cancellation of cross terms under the independent signs; no cross-pairing is assumed to vanish. By <a href=\"#eq:completed-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:completed-norm\">[eq:completed-norm]</a>, $$\\max_{\\epsilon_i\\in\\{-1,1\\}}\n       \\left\\|\\sum_{i\\in H}\\epsilon_i m_i\\right\\|_Z^2\n \\geq\\mathbb E\\left\\|\\sum_{i\\in H}\\epsilon_i m_i\\right\\|_Z^2\n \\geq\\alpha_d|H|\\delta^2.$$ Taking square roots proves <a href=\"#eq:detector-growth\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:detector-growth\">[eq:detector-growth]</a>. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-324.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-detector-growth",
      "inputs": [
        "Compatible detectors",
        "A common positive detection bound",
        "Exact list formula"
      ],
      "method": "Average over independent signs so cross terms cancel and retain the diagonal pairings.",
      "output": "Growth of maximum signed-sum norms.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-detector-growth"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-compatible",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-compatible",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-compatible</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Fix a finite set of indices and signs. In the first case, at a given point at most one of the corresponding closed supports contains that point. All other summands vanish on a relative neighborhood, so the sum is locally either zero or a single signed $F_i$, with bound $L$.\n\nIn the second case, choose at a given point the largest selected index $j$ whose $u_j$-support contains the point. If there is no such index, every summand vanishes on a common neighborhood, because there are only finitely many closed supports. Otherwise all selected later functions vanish on a neighborhood. The point lies in $C_i$ for each selected $i<j$, so on a smaller neighborhood all those $u_i$ equal $e_i$. On that neighborhood the sum is $$\\epsilon_jF_j+\\sum_{\\substack{i<j\\\\i\\text{ selected}}}\n                                  \\epsilon_i e_i b_i,$$ and has the asserted local Lipschitz bound.\n\nTo pass from the uniform local bound to a global bound, take any two points of $B$. The segment between them is contained in $B$. Its compact parameter interval has a finite cover by the relative neighborhoods just obtained and hence a Lebesgue number. Subdivide the segment finely enough that each subsegment lies in one such neighborhood, apply the local estimate to each subsegment, and add. The lengths of the collinear subsegments sum to the distance of the endpoints. This proves both global assertions. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-324.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-support-compatibility",
      "inputs": [
        "Uniform individual Lipschitz bounds",
        "Disjoint supports or summably weighted nested plateaux"
      ],
      "method": "Establish local Lipschitz bounds from disjoint supports or nested plateaux, then subdivide segments in the convex domain.",
      "output": "Uniform Lipschitz bounds for all finite signed sums.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-compatible"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-tail-separation",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-test-duality"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-tail-separation",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-tail-separation</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Choose a dyadic $c=2^{-a}$ with $2c<c_*$. If the original class is $\\mathcal T_h$, view a test as a cylindrical factor with no tail factors; if it is $\\mathcal V_h$, keep its chosen product representation. A single integer $H$, larger than $h$, the bounded band indices, and $a$, can be chosen so that both $F_i$ and $F_i b_{c,N}$ belong to $\\mathcal V_H$ for all $i,N$. There is room for one extra tail factor.\n\nFor each fixed $i$, $b_{c,N}(s)\\to1$ at every $s\\in B$, since $\\tau_N(s)\\to0$. Lemma <a href=\"#lem:duality\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:duality\">12</a>, used in this one class, therefore gives $\\langle F_i b_{c,N},m_i\\rangle\\to\\langle F_i,m_i\\rangle$. Choose $N$ large enough that the difference has modulus less than $\\delta/2$; every sufficiently large $N$ then works. This choice is made for the fixed vector $m_i$.\n\nSelect indices recursively. After choosing an index $i$ and such an $N_i$, take the next original index so far out that its $M_k$ exceeds all previously selected $N_i$, which is possible because $M_k\\to\\infty$. Then choose its own $N_k$ as above. For selected $k>i$, the new $i$-th support requires $\\tau_{N_i}\\leq2c$ by <a href=\"#eq:tail-support\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:tail-support\">[eq:tail-support]</a>. The new $k$-th support requires $\\tau_{M_k}\\geq c_*$ by <a href=\"#eq:tail-hypotheses\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:tail-hypotheses\">[eq:tail-hypotheses]</a>, hence $\\tau_{N_i}\\geq\\tau_{M_k}\\geq c_*>2c$. The supports are disjoint, and the detection bound follows from the choice of $N_i$. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-324.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-tail-separation",
      "inputs": [

      "output": "Disjoint modified detectors in one enlarged class.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-tail-separation"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-no-c0",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-no-c0",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-prop-no-c0</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Suppose $T:c_0\\to Z$ is an isomorphic embedding and put $m_i=Te_i$. The lower bound for $T$ and the identity $\\|\\sum_{i\\in H}\\epsilon_i e_i\\|_\\infty=1$ for nonempty finite $H$ give <a href=\"#eq:bounded-signed\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:bounded-signed\">[eq:bounded-signed]</a> for some $r,M>0$. Lemma <a href=\"#lem:detectors\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:detectors\">15</a> supplies a weakly null subsequence and detectors in one class. Proposition <a href=\"#prop:compatible-detectors\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:compatible-detectors\">19</a> then supplies compatible detectors with a common nonzero pairing. For their class $\\mathcal L_1$, bound $C_1$, and pairing constant $\\delta_1$, set $d_1=(\\mathcal L_1,C_1,2)$. Lemma <a href=\"#lem:detector-growth\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:detector-growth\">16</a> gives $$M^2\\geq\\alpha_{d_1}|H|\\delta_1^2$$ for every finite set of selected indices $H$, a contradiction. Thus no such embedding $T$ exists. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-324.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-exclude-linear-copy",
      "inputs": [
        "An assumed linear isomorphic embedding",
        "Uniform signed-sum bounds of its basis image"
      ],

Proposition <a href=\"#prop:no-c0\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:no-c0\">20</a> excludes a linearly isomorphic copy of ordinary $c_0$ in $Z$. Propositions <a href=\"#prop:global\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:global\">6</a> and <a href=\"#prop:onto\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:onto\">7</a> give a bi-Lipschitz bijection $$g:E\\oplus_\\infty U\\longrightarrow U$$ with $E=c_0(\\mathbb N)$ and lower constant $c_g=1/5-24\\eta>0$. Lemmas <a href=\"#lem:Q\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:Q\">13</a> and <a href=\"#lem:K\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:K\">14</a> give a bounded linear $Q:Z\\to U$ and a Lipschitz $K:E\\oplus_\\infty U\\to Z$ with $\\operatorname{Lip}(K)\\leq3$ and $QK=g-P$. Applying Lemma <a href=\"#lem:absorption\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:absorption\">2</a> yields the onto bi-Lipschitz map $$F:Z\\oplus_\\infty E\\to Z,\\qquad\n             F(b,t)=b+K(t,Qb),$$ with the explicit inverse <a href=\"#eq:absorb-inverse\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:absorb-inverse\">[eq:absorb-inverse]</a>. Its inverse is the bi-Lipschitz bijection in the opposite direction $Z\\to Z\\oplus_\\infty c_0$. This proves the theorem. ◻",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-metric-linear-distinction**: The constructed separable real Banach space absorbs the direct summand bi-Lipschitzly while containing no linearly isomorphic copy of that summand.
- **invariant-lifted-correction**: The absorption map is a bi-Lipschitz bijection when the correction identity holds exactly and the auxiliary nonlinear shift is a bi-Lipschitz bijection. The linear correction operator need not be onto.
- **invariant-state-dependent-distance-control**: The nonlinear shift retains global upper and positive lower distance bounds through control of weight variation. Inverting a frozen shift alone does not invert the nonlinear shift.
- **invariant-terminal-scalar-surjectivity**: Surjectivity requires a separate argument: finite rows retain terminal scalars, the fixed-point equation sets them to zero, and density together with a closed image yields onto-ness.
- **invariant-ordered-strict-scale-budget**: Generated tests retain localized supports when their operation scales are nonincreasing and their total scale remains strictly below the prescribed budget. The strict budget permits a further small operation.
- **invariant-fixed-vector-limit**: Pointwise convergence of tests from a fixed class gives convergence of pairings with each fixed vector in the completed space. The class supplies the common dual bound needed for this passage.
- **invariant-compatible-detector-growth**: Detectors in a common class force growth of vector signed sums only after their own finite signed sums have a common Lipschitz bound. Disjoint supports or the stated nested plateau conditions provide compatibility.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-metric-implies-linear**: Infer a linear subspace embedding from the existence of a bi-Lipschitz embedding. -> The constructed space admits the metric embedding while excluding the corresponding linear copy.
- **anti-use-quotient-section**: Interpret the lifted correction as a Lipschitz right inverse of the linear correction operator. -> The identity lifts a correction term and does not establish a right inverse or surjectivity of the linear operator.
- **anti-use-frozen-inverse**: Apply the frozen linear inverse as an inverse of the normalized nonlinear shift. -> The weights are recomputed from the input, so a frozen inverse does not solve the nonlinear inverse problem.
- **anti-use-lower-bound-as-onto-proof**: Treat the positive lower Lipschitz bound as proof of onto-ness. -> The lower bound establishes injectivity and closedness of the image without supplying a preimage of every target.
- **anti-use-unordered-scales**: Allow arbitrary operation order whenever the total scale budget is bounded. -> Later operations could enlarge the support after the first small remainder, defeating the shrinking-support argument.
- **anti-use-moving-vector-limit**: Use pointwise test convergence as uniform convergence of pairings along a moving sequence of vectors. -> The molecule approximation and the convergence index may depend on the fixed vector.
- **anti-use-detectors-without-compatibility**: Conclude signed-sum growth from individual nonzero detector pairings alone. -> The detector list need not be admissible without a common Lipschitz bound for all its signed sums.
- **anti-use-support-modulo-constants**: Replace generated tests by representatives modulo constants when reasoning about supports. -> Although constants vanish on molecules, changing a representative can change its support and its plateau value.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result constructs an infinite-dimensional Banach completion and excludes a linear subspace through subsequence selection and arbitrarily large finite signed sums. Surjectivity also uses the existence statement “Brouwer’s fixed-point theorem gives $x=T(x)$.” The construction explicitly takes a Banach completion: “Let $N$ be its kernel, and let $Z$ be the Banach completion of $\mathcal M_0(B)/N$ with the induced norm.” These passages provide no finite procedure deciding the completed-space theorem. Finite checks of frozen block formulas would cover an auxiliary identity only and would not certify surjectivity of the nonlinear shift or the absence of a linear copy.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The target space is obtained by quotienting a molecule space and taking its Banach completion. The paper supplies no finite representation of arbitrary completed elements or executable interface for the resulting equivalence. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The frozen shift replaces scalar readouts while preserving the corresponding kernel components. This is an operator construction on sequence spaces, with no simulation state evolution, collision model, or physical update contract. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The absorption criterion provides an inverse formula conditional on an available bi-Lipschitz bijection and an exact lifted correction. It does not provide procedures for evaluating those objects on finite encodings. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Surjectivity uses a continuous self-map of a finite-dimensional ball and Brouwer's fixed-point theorem, followed by density and closedness in the infinite-dimensional target. This existence argument supplies no terminating preimage algorithm or approximation guarantee. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite coordinate dependence localizes each correction function, but the complete family ranges over countably many blocks and feeds an infinite-dimensional space. Local finiteness alone supplies no storage layout, update scheme, or finite query contract. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The weight construction controls analytic norms, continuity, and variation with the local radius. It supplies no arithmetic precision contract, parallel execution design, or hardware resource bounds. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The global distance bounds establish injectivity and continuity of the shift. They do not establish computational hardness, secrecy, or resistance to an adversary. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The universal embedding consequence concerns existence of bi-Lipschitz images of separable metric spaces in the constructed Banach space. It supplies no trainable parameterization, learning objective, or finite feature representation. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The local operations flatten functions on coordinate cylinders and control the remainder and its support. These analytic transformations are not an optimization problem and carry no objective improvement or feasibility certificate. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Compatibility bounds finite signed sums of functions through disjoint supports or nested regions of constancy. It is a geometric property and does not define agreement, communication, or fault handling among distributed participants. |
| other (Functional analysis and metric geometry) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The paper constructs a specific separable real Banach space from selected tests and mass-zero molecules, then combines a lifted correction with an onto shift to obtain absorption while excluding a linear copy of the absorbed sequence space. This is a concrete mathematical construction whose completion and existence arguments do not supply a finite executable implementation. |

Let $N$ be its kernel, and let $Z$ be the Banach completion of $\\mathcal M_0(B)/N$ with the induced norm. Then $Z$ is separable, and $$\\zeta:B\\to Z,\\qquad \\zeta(s)=[\\delta_s-\\delta_0]             \\label{eq:zeta}$$ is $1$-Lipschitz with $\\zeta(0)=0$. Each $\\sigma_d$ descends and extends continuously to a seminorm on $Z$, and the exact formula $$\\|z\\|_Z^2=\\sum_{d\\geq1}\n                                \\alpha_d\\sigma_d(z)^2         \\label{eq:completed-norm}$$ holds for every $z\\in Z$.",
          "source_object": "Banach completion of the molecule quotient"
        }

Assume the exact identity $$QK(t,u)=g(t,u)-u\n                 \\qquad ((t,u)\\in D).                    \\label{eq:absorb-identity}$$ Then $$F:Z\\oplus_\\infty E\\longrightarrow Z,\\qquad\n F(b,t)=b+K(t,Qb)$$ is a bi-Lipschitz bijection. If $$q=\\|Q\\|,\\qquad L_K=\\operatorname{Lip}(K),\\qquad\n \\|g(x)-g(x')\\|_U\\geq c_g\\|x-x'\\|_D$$ for some arbitrary lower constant $c_g>0$, then $$\\operatorname{Lip}(F)\\leq 1+L_K\\max\\{1,q\\},\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Lip}(F^{-1})\\leq\n \\max\\left\\{1+\\frac{L_Kq}{c_g},\\frac{q}{c_g}\\right\\}.     \\label{eq:absorb-bounds}$$ No surjectivity or right-inverse assumption is imposed on $Q$.",

          "anchor": "eq:brouwer-map",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0\\ne y\\in U_N$, and set $h=\\|y\\|_U>0$. On the closed ball $\\{x\\in D_N:\\|x\\|_D\\leq5h\\}$, define $$s(x)=\\frac{x}{\\max\\{\\|x\\|_D,h/6\\}},\\qquad\n T(x)=(S_{s(x)}^{(N)})^{-1}(y,0).                            \\label{eq:brouwer-map}$$ The denominator is positive even at $x=0$, so $s(x)\\in B$ depends continuously on $x$. Equations <a href=\"#eq:finite-inverse\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-inverse\">[eq:finite-inverse]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:finite-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-bounds\">[eq:finite-bounds]</a> show that $T$ is a continuous self-map of this finite-dimensional closed ball. Brouwer’s fixed-point theorem gives $x=T(x)$. At that fixed point, $$h=\\|(y,0)\\|=\\|S_{s(x)}^{(N)}x\\|\\leq5\\|x\\|_D,$$ and therefore $\\|x\\|_D\\geq h/5>h/6$. The state in <a href=\"#eq:brouwer-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:brouwer-map\">[eq:brouwer-map]</a> is now the normalized state $s_x$, not the regularized state used near zero. The blocks of $g(x)$ in the $N$-square are $y$, and <a href=\"#eq:finite-shift\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-shift\">[eq:finite-shift]</a> gives $a_{i,N}=0$ for every $i\\leq N$.",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-real-separable-existence** (formal_scope): The existence theorem concerns a particular separable real Banach space. It does not assert absorption for an arbitrary Banach space or establish the analogous complex-space statement. Blocked use: ['Apply the existence conclusion to an arbitrary pre-existing Banach space.', 'Extend the construction to complex scalars without an additional argument.']
- **limit-metric-control-does-not-preserve-linearity** (counterexample_scope): Bi-Lipschitz equivalence and metric embeddings do not imply the corresponding linear isomorphism or linear subspace embedding in this construction. Blocked use: ['Recover a linear copy from the metric image of the null-sequence space.', 'Treat the absorption bijection as preserving addition.']
- **limit-exact-absorption-preconditions** (hypothesis): The absorption criterion requires a bounded linear correction operator, a Lipschitz lift, a bi-Lipschitz bijection for the auxiliary shift, and the exact correction identity. An approximate identity is outside the stated conclusion. Blocked use: ['Apply the inverse formula when the auxiliary shift is only injective.', 'Replace the exact lifted correction by a numerically small residual while retaining the asserted bijection.']
- **limit-correction-lift-is-not-a-section** (formal_scope): The lifted correction supplies neither a surjectivity assertion for the linear operator nor a right inverse of that operator. Blocked use: ['Infer a linear splitting from the correction identity.', 'Assume arbitrary target values have preimages under the linear correction operator.']
- **limit-weight-variation-and-localisation** (hypothesis): The replacement weights must satisfy the normalized pairing, uniform norm bounds, radius-weighted variation estimate, and band support restrictions. Arbitrary continuous weights are not covered by the distance and support arguments. Blocked use: ['Substitute continuous weights without the radius-weighted variation estimate.', 'Use the later support thresholds for weights lacking the stated band restrictions.']
- **limit-local-finite-dependence-is-not-global-finiteness** (formal_scope): Finite dependence is asserted for each correction coordinate separately. It does not provide a common finite coordinate set representing the entire construction. Blocked use: ['Replace the full correction family by a fixed finite collection and claim the whole-space theorem.', 'Infer a finite representation of arbitrary completed-space elements from coordinatewise finite dependence.']
- **limit-frozen-inverse-does-not-invert-state-dependence** (hypothesis): The explicit linear inverse applies when the weight pairs are fixed. The nonlinear shift recomputes its weights from the normalized input, so its inverse requires a separate argument. Blocked use: ['Freeze weights at an output and use the frozen inverse as the nonlinear inverse.', 'Infer nonlinear surjectivity directly from the frozen operator bounds.']
- **limit-brouwer-existence-is-not-an-inversion-algorithm** (nonconstructive): The nonlinear surjectivity proof obtains a fixed point by Brouwer's theorem and extends finite-target solvability through density and closedness. It supplies no terminating inversion procedure or convergence rate for fixed-point iteration. Blocked use: ['Advertise the fixed-point self-map as a proved convergent numerical solver.', 'Use finite agreement to certify surjectivity on the complete target space.']
- **limit-terminal-data-and-spillover** (edge_case): The finite-row inverse retains independent terminal scalar data. In the surjectivity argument those scalars are set to zero to eliminate the possible output in the next block. Blocked use: ['Delete the terminal output coordinates while retaining the finite inverse claim.', 'Embed a finite-row solution into the infinite target without checking outgoing spillover.']
- **limit-local-operation-input-contract** (hypothesis): The local operations act on a Lipschitz function depending on its specified finite coordinate set, with a permitted grid center and dyadic scale. Their support estimates do not justify applying the same operations to an unrestricted tail-dependent full test. Blocked use: ['Apply a cylindrical operation directly to the full tail-dependent product during the repeated-label expansion.', 'Use the support-enlargement conclusion without preserving the coordinate restriction defining the operation.']
- **limit-ordered-strict-scale-budget** (hypothesis): The operation scales must be nonincreasing and their total must remain strictly below the prescribed budget. The order controls support shrinkage after a small remainder, while strictness leaves room for an additional local modification. Blocked use: ['Replace the strict budget by equality when a further operation is needed.', 'Reorder the scales arbitrarily while claiming the repeated-label shrinking-support argument.']
- **limit-supports-use-concrete-representatives** (formal_scope): Support and plateau arguments concern the actual generated functions. Although constants vanish in molecule pairings, changing a function by a constant can change the support and plateau information used in compatibility. Blocked use: ['Replace tests by arbitrary representatives modulo constants during support propagation.', 'Infer geometric compatibility solely from equality of molecule functionals.']
- **limit-fixed-class-bounds-are-not-uniform-across-classes** (hypothesis): The test bounds are uniform within each indicated class and depend on its operation and tail-factor budget. They do not provide a common bound across all classes. Blocked use: ['Allow unbounded operation depth while retaining a fixed class bound.', 'Move through different test classes in a pointwise-limit argument without establishing a common dual bound.']
- **limit-quotient-completion-and-exact-norm** (nonconstructive): The initial formula is a seminorm, so its kernel must be quotiented before Banach completion. The completed norm uses an infinite weighted sum of seminorms defined by suprema over admissible lists; no finite norm-evaluation procedure is supplied. Blocked use: ['Treat distinct formal molecules as necessarily distinct completed-space elements.', 'Treat a finite truncation of the norm formula as an exact norm on the completed space.', 'Claim finite executable certification of the full theorem from auxiliary block identities.']
- **limit-pointwise-duality-fixes-the-vector** (hypothesis): Pointwise convergence of tests gives convergence of pairings in a fixed class for each fixed completed-space vector. The molecule approximation and convergence index may depend on that vector. Blocked use: ['Pass to a limit uniformly along a moving sequence of vectors.', 'Choose all localization scales from pointwise convergence without fixing the vector whose pairing must be retained.']
- **limit-correction-target-is-the-null-sequence-space** (hypothesis): The linear correction map takes values in the complete null-sequence target because each correction vector already belongs to that target and finite molecule sums remain there. Coordinatewise boundedness alone would not establish this conclusion. Blocked use: ['Replace the target by bounded sequences and assume the null-sequence conclusion follows.', 'Extend coordinate functionals separately without checking that their assembled values lie in the required target.']
- **limit-radial-lift-uses-normalised-states** (edge_case): The radial lift defines the zero input separately and evaluates the point map at normalized nonzero inputs. The correction on the interior of the ball uses the unnormalized state and cannot simply be identified with the homogeneous nonlinear correction there. Blocked use: ['Evaluate the normalization formula at zero.', 'Substitute the homogeneous shift correction for the state-based ball correction at every interior point.']
- **limit-detector-extraction-needs-signed-sum-bounds** (hypothesis): Detector extraction starts with vectors bounded away from zero and uniformly bounded finite signed sums. Weak nullity alone does not supply the detector conclusion of that lemma. Blocked use: ['Apply detector extraction to an arbitrary weakly null sequence.', 'Replace uniform signed-sum bounds by bounds on individual vectors only.']
- **limit-detection-needs-compatibility-for-growth** (hypothesis): Individual nonzero pairings force the stated signed-sum growth only when the tests belong to a common listed class and all their finite signed sums share a Lipschitz bound. Cross-pairings need not vanish. Blocked use: ['Apply the growth estimate to an incompatible list of individually bounded tests.', 'Require vanishing cross-pairings as if they were an assumption of the random-sign argument.']
- **limit-geometric-compatibility-preconditions** (hypothesis): The compatibility lemma assumes a convex domain and either disjoint closed relative supports or nested supports inside regions of constancy. With nonconstant tail factors, the weighted plateau contributions must be summable. Blocked use: ['Infer a global signed-sum bound from local bounds on an arbitrary nonconvex domain.', 'Use nesting of full product supports where the argument requires nesting of cylindrical supports.', 'Discard the summability condition for nonconstant tail factors.']
- **limit-tail-separation-threshold-and-bands** (hypothesis): Tail separation requires bounded representing band indices, a common positive detection bound, and supports in escaping tails with a common positive threshold. These conditions permit the modified tests to remain in a single enlarged class. Blocked use: ['Use the tail-separation lemma when the lower tail threshold tends to zero.', 'Ignore unbounded representing bands when choosing the enlarged class.']
- **limit-compatible-selection-is-subsequential** (nonconstructive): Compatible-detector selection assumes weak nullity and detection in a fixed class, and its conclusion permits a subsequence and a changed class. It does not make the original detectors compatible or provide an effective uniform selection procedure. Blocked use: ['Claim compatibility of the original full detector sequence.', 'Remove weak nullity when discarding fixed terms in the repeated-label expansion.', 'Infer an executable selection algorithm from the compactness and fixed-vector choices.']
- **limit-universality-is-metric-and-separable** (formal_scope): The universal-target consequence concerns bi-Lipschitz embeddings of separable metric spaces and uses the cited embedding theorem through composition. It does not supply linear embeddings, an effective embedding procedure for arbitrary inputs, or a nonseparable universality conclusion. Blocked use: ['Extend the universal embedding conclusion to all metric spaces.', 'Interpret the universal target as a finite-dimensional feature representation.', 'Claim a computational construction for arbitrary metric inputs from the corollary alone.']

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-onto",
          "quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-prop-onto</summary>\n\n\n*Proof.* For $N\\geq1$, let $U_N$ consist of the blocks with $1\\leq i,n\\leq N$, all other coordinates zero. Let $D_N$ consist of these blocks and $t_1,\\ldots,t_N$, all other input coordinates zero. They are finite-dimensional, and $$\\dim D_N=N+\\sum_{i,n\\leq N}|\\Gamma_{i,n}|\n          =\\dim(U_N\\oplus_\\infty\\mathbb R^N).                       \\label{eq:finite-dimensions}$$ For $s\\in B$, set $$S_s^{(N)}:D_N\\to U_N\\oplus_\\infty\\mathbb R^N,\\qquad\n S_s^{(N)}x=\n \\left(\\bigl((S_sx)^{i,n}\\bigr)_{i,n\\leq N},\n       \\bigl(w^{i,N}(s)x^{i,N}\\bigr)_{i\\leq N}\\right).        \\label{eq:finite-shift}$$ The last $N$ coordinates retain the terminal scalars $a_{i,N}$.\n\n\nFor clarity, this finite map has an explicit inverse. Given $(y,(c_i)_{i\\leq N})$, define $$b_{i,n}=w^{i,n}(s)y^{i,n},\\qquad\n a_{i,0}=b_{i,1},\\qquad\n a_{i,n}=b_{i,n+1}\\ (n<N),\\qquad a_{i,N}=c_i,$$ then put $$t_i=a_{i,0},\\qquad\n x^{i,n}=y^{i,n}\n          -v^{i,n}(s)(a_{i,n-1}-a_{i,n})\\quad(n\\leq N).      \\label{eq:finite-inverse}$$ Because $a_{i,n-1}=b_{i,n}$, applying $w^{i,n}(s)$ to <a href=\"#eq:finite-inverse\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-inverse\">[eq:finite-inverse]</a> gives $w^{i,n}(s)x^{i,n}=a_{i,n}$. Substitution in <a href=\"#eq:finite-shift\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-shift\">[eq:finite-shift]</a> returns $(y,c)$. Conversely, applying the rows to <a href=\"#eq:finite-shift\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-shift\">[eq:finite-shift]</a> recovers the $b_{i,n}$, and <a href=\"#eq:finite-inverse\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-inverse\">[eq:finite-inverse]</a> recovers every input coordinate. Thus <a href=\"#eq:finite-inverse\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-inverse\">[eq:finite-inverse]</a> is the inverse.\n\n\nThe same estimates as for the frozen shift give $$\\|S_s^{(N)}\\|\\leq5,\\qquad\n                  \\|(S_s^{(N)})^{-1}\\|\\leq5.                \\label{eq:finite-bounds}$$ For the inverse bound, if $h_0=\\|(y,c)\\|$, then $|b_{i,n}|\\leq2h_0$, all $a_{i,n}$ have modulus at most $2h_0$, and <a href=\"#eq:finite-inverse\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-inverse\">[eq:finite-inverse]</a> has block norm at most $h_0+4h_0$. For the forward bound, the block estimate is $5\\|x\\|_D$ and the terminal coordinates are at most $2\\|x\\|_D$. The finitely many weights in <a href=\"#eq:finite-inverse\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-inverse\">[eq:finite-inverse]</a> are continuous in $s$. Hence $s\\mapsto(S_s^{(N)})^{-1}$ is continuous as a map into the linear operators between these finite-dimensional spaces.\n\nFigure <a href=\"#fig:terminal\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"fig:terminal\">1</a> displays the terminal coordinate and the single possible outgoing block of a finite row.\n\n<figure id=\"fig:terminal\">\n\n<figcaption>A row of length $N$ with frozen weights and independent extra target scalar $c_{i} = a_{i, N}$. The block readouts recover the predecessor scalars, while $c_{i}$ records the scalar not read by any block in the finite row. At the nonlinear fixed point it is set to zero, which kills the only possible output spillover into the next block.</figcaption>\n</figure>\n\n\n**Figure source and visual witness** output\\\\\\(v^{i,N+1}c_i\\)};\n\\draw[->] (terminal) -- node[right,align=left]\n {\\(x^{i,N+1}=0\\)} (spill);\n\\node[align=left,anchor=west] at (0,-1.8)\n {Finite target: \\((y,c)\\).\\\\\n  Brouwer is applied with \\(c=0\\).};\n\\end{tikzpicture}\n```\n\nVisual witness description of PDF page 10 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):\n\n```text\nA horizontal schematic pipeline of rectangular boxes connected by arrows:\n- First box: \"$a_{i,0} = t_i$\"\n- Horizontal arrow pointing right to the second box: contains \"$y^{i,1}$\" and \"$w^{i,1}y^{i,1} = a_{i,0}$\"\n- Horizontal $h=\\|y\\|_U>0$. On the closed ball $\\{x\\in D_N:\\|x\\|_D\\leq5h\\}$, define $$s(x)=\\frac{x}{\\max\\{\\|x\\|_D,h/6\\}},\\qquad\n T(x)=(S_{s(x)}^{(N)})^{-1}(y,0).                            \\label{eq:brouwer-map}$$ The denominator is positive even at $x=0$, so $s(x)\\in B$ depends continuously on $x$. Equations <a href=\"#eq:finite-inverse\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-inverse\">[eq:finite-inverse]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:finite-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-bounds\">[eq:finite-bounds]</a> show that $T$ is a continuous self-map of this finite-dimensional closed ball. Brouwer’s fixed-point theorem gives $x=T(x)$. At that fixed point, $$h=\\|(y,0)\\|=\\|S_{s(x)}^{(N)}x\\|\\leq5\\|x\\|_D,$$ and therefore $\\|x\\|_D\\geq h/5>h/6$. There $$(g(x))^{i,N+1}\n       =v^{i,N+1}(s_x)a_{i,N}=0.$$ All other outside blocks have zero input and zero predecessor scalar. Thus $g(x)=y$ in $U$. The zero target is $g(0)$, so $U_N\\subset g(D)$ for every $N$.\n\nEvery finitely supported element of $U$ belongs to some $U_N$, and such elements are dense. Hence $g(D)$ is both dense and closed in $U$, and is all of $U$. ◻",

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 324 (`lean/docs/324.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Lipschitz Equivalent Separable Banach Spaces Need Not Be Linearly Isomorphic](../../preprints/Lipschitz-Equivalent-Separable-Banach-Spaces-Need-Not-Be-Linearly-Isomorphic-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Bi-Lipschitz Absorption of $c_0$ Without a Linear Copy of $c_0$](../../preprints/Bi-Lipschitz-Absorption-of-c0-Without-a-Linear-Copy-of-c0-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized counterexample gives separable real Banach spaces $X,Y$ that are bi-Lipschitz equivalent but not linearly isomorphic. The bijection has lower Lipschitz bound $4/21$ and upper bound $76/25$. The linear obstruction is explicit: $X$ contains a linear isometric copy of $c_0(\ell_2)$, whereas $Y$ contains no bounded linear copy of that space.
>
> The formalized result constructs one separable real Banach space $X$ that is bi-Lipschitz equivalent to $X\times c_0$ but contains no closed linear subspace isomorphic to $c_0$. The same space contains a bi-Lipschitz copy of $c_0$, is bi-Lipschitz universal for separable metric spaces, and is not linearly isomorphic to $X\times c_0$. Thus nonlinear absorption of $c_0$ does not force a linear copy of it.

Comparator source `C0Absorption.lean` (1057 bytes, sha256 `a5d7375b335f7dd950c879f1d99d60cda553d2a4d7e68b0170a35c5657becdbe`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace C0Absorption

open scoped ZeroAtInfty

abbrev C0 := C₀(ℕ, ℝ)

def BiLip {X Y : Type*} [MetricSpace X] [MetricSpace Y] (f : X → Y) : Prop :=
  ∃ c C : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ c ≤ C ∧
    ∀ x y, c * dist x y ≤ dist (f x) (f y) ∧ dist (f x) (f y) ≤ C * dist x y

def NoLinearC0 (Z : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup Z] [NormedSpace ℝ Z] : Prop :=
  ∀ T : C0 →L[ℝ] Z, ¬ ∃ c : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ ∀ x, c * ‖x‖ ≤ ‖T x‖

def MetricUniversal (Z : Type*) [MetricSpace Z] : Prop :=
  ∀ (M : Type) [MetricSpace M] [TopologicalSpace.SeparableSpace M],
    ∃ f : M → Z, BiLip f

def MainConclusion : Prop :=
  ∃ (Z : Type) (_ : NormedAddCommGroup Z) (_ : NormedSpace ℝ Z),
    CompleteSpace Z ∧ TopologicalSpace.SeparableSpace Z ∧ NoLinearC0 Z ∧
    (∃ F : Z × C0 → Z, Function.Surjective F ∧ BiLip F) ∧
    (∃ f : C0 → Z, BiLip f) ∧ MetricUniversal Z ∧
    ¬ Nonempty ((Z × C0) ≃L[ℝ] Z)

theorem main_result : MainConclusion := by sorry

end C0Absorption

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a separable real Banach space <i>Z</i> that contains no linear copy of <i>c</i><sub>0</sub>, yet is bi-Lipschitz equivalent to $`Z\oplus_\infty c_0`$. The same space contains a bi-Lipschitz image of every separable metric space.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

All Banach spaces in this paper are real. A map $F:X\to Y$ is a *bi-Lipschitz embedding* if there are constants $0<c\leq C<\infty$ such that $$c\|x-x'\|_X\leq\|F(x)-F(x')\|_Y
       \leq C\|x-x'\|_X\qquad(x,x'\in X).$$ It is a *bi-Lipschitz equivalence* when it is also onto $Y$. The distinction between these two notions is central here: the result constructs an onto map between whole Banach spaces, while its linear obstruction concerns a subspace.

The nonlinear geometry of Banach spaces asks how much linear structure is retained by maps that control distances but need not respect addition. Ribe proved that uniformly homeomorphic normed spaces have the same finite-dimensional linear structure up to one common distortion bound (Ribe 1976, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Ribe76](#ref-Ribe76)]. Aharoni and Lindenstrauss constructed nonseparable Banach spaces that are bi-Lipschitz equivalent but not linearly isomorphic, and asked for separable examples (Aharoni and Lindenstrauss 1978, 281–82) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[AL78](#ref-AL78)].
